
1. Data Structure & Placement
You must generate rows using the specific sub-query format below to link Opportunities to Career Paths.

Target Table Scope: student_opportunities Input Format: INSERT INTO ... SELECT ... pattern.

2. Inflexible Constraints (MUST FOLLOW)
The following data points have ZERO FLEXIBILITY. You must adhere strictly to these formats or the import will fail.

career_id: NEVER use a raw UUID. You MUST use the sub-query: 
(SELECT id FROM career_paths WHERE path_name = 'EXACT_CAREER_NAME_HERE' LIMIT 1)
type (Enum): Must be exactly one of: 'job', 'exam', 'course', 'scholarship'.
risk_level: Must be 'Low', 'Medium', or 'High'.
allowed_grades (JSON): Must be a valid JSON array containing one or more of the following standard values. You can mix and match.
School: "5th Grade"..."12th Grade (Science - PCM)", "12th Grade (Commerce)", etc.
Diploma: "Diploma in Engineering (Polytechnic)", "ITI Certificate", "Professional Certification"
Undergraduate: "B.Tech / B.E (Computer Science)", "MBBS (Medicine)", "B.Com (Honours)", "BA (Psychology)", "BBA (Management)", etc. (All major degrees)
Postgraduate: "M.Tech", "MBA / PGDM", "MD / MS", "M.Sc", etc.
Others: "PhD / Doctorate", "Chartered Accountant (CA)", "Gap Year"
Example: '["12th Grade (Science - PCM)", "Gap Year", "B.Tech / B.E (Computer Science)"]'
roadmap_steps (JSON): Must be a valid JSON array of objects with keys: phase, duration, action.
Example: '[{"phase": "Prep", "duration": "2 Months", "action": "Study NCERT"}]'
3. Data Volume & Quality
Volume: Generate 5 distinct opportunities for the specified Career Path.
Quality:
fit_analysis should be psychological and personalized (e.g., "Good for students who...").
trait_requirements (JSON): Must be a valid JSON object.
Allowed Keys (ONLY use these 6):
analytical_tendency (Logic, Data, Math)
social_orientation (People interaction, Leadership)
learning_patience (Long-term study vs fast results)
risk_tolerance (High stakes vs safety)
stability_preference (Predictable routine vs dynamic change)
authority_comfort (Following rules vs independence)
Value: Float between 0.0 and 1.0.
Example: '{"analytical_tendency": 0.8, "social_orientation": 0.3}'
4. Output Template
Use this EXACT SQL structure for your output:

INSERT INTO student_opportunities (id, career_id, title, type, risk_level, description, allowed_grades, trait_requirements, fit_analysis, roadmap_steps, created_at, updated_at)
SELECT 
    UUID(),
    id, -- Auto-linked to career
    '[Title]',
    '[job|exam|course|scholarship]',
    '[Low|Medium|High]',
    '[Description]',
    '[JSON Array of Grades]',
    '[JSON Object of Traits]',
    '[Fit Analysis Text]',
    '[JSON Array of Roadmap Steps]',
    NOW(),
    NOW()
FROM career_paths 
WHERE path_name = '[Target Career Name]' -- e.g., 'Software Engineer (Product)'
LIMIT 1;

CREATE TABLE `student_opportunities` (
  `id` varchar(36) NOT NULL,
  `career_id` varchar(36) NOT NULL,
  `title` varchar(255) NOT NULL,
  `type` enum('job','exam','course','scholarship') NOT NULL,
  `risk_level` varchar(50) DEFAULT NULL,
  `description` text DEFAULT NULL,
  `allowed_grades` longtext CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE utf8mb4_bin DEFAULT NULL CHECK (json_valid(`allowed_grades`)),
  `trait_requirements` longtext CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE utf8mb4_bin DEFAULT NULL CHECK (json_valid(`trait_requirements`)),
  `fit_analysis` text DEFAULT NULL,
  `roadmap_steps` longtext CHARACTER SET utf8mb4 COLLATE utf8mb4_bin DEFAULT NULL CHECK (json_valid(`roadmap_steps`)),
  `created_at` timestamp NOT NULL DEFAULT current_timestamp(),
  `updated_at` timestamp NOT NULL DEFAULT current_timestamp() ON UPDATE current_timestamp()
) ENGINE=InnoDB DEFAULT CHARSET=utf8mb4 COLLATE=utf8mb4_general_ci;

and counselling mechanisms in every school to assist the students and
the parents. These counselling cells are tasked with implementing a
system to address students’ aspirations and help them pursue their
choices and passion.
The Ministry of Education, Government of India has collaborated
with United Nations Children's Fund (UNICEF}, New Delhi to come out
with a comprehensive "Career Guidance Book", which contains 500
career cards. |t is a testament to our commitment to provide every
student with the tools and knowledge necessary to make informed
decisions about their future career. Providing a snapshot of different
professions, outlining key responsibilities, required qualifications, and
potential career trajectories, these career cards are designed to be
user-friendly, making career exploration an engaging and infomative
experience for our students.
lam happy that the “Career Guidance Book", featuring career
cards compiled into two volumes, is being launched on the occasion
of the 4th Anniversary of National Education Policy 2020.
| invite all our students to benefit from the career guide and
utilize the information effectively in their pursuit of a successful career.

MESSAGE

It gives me immense pleasure to know that the PSS Central Institute of
Vocational Education (PSSCIVE) in collaboration with the United Nations
Children’s Fund (UNICEF), India has developed two volumes of the
comprehensive “Career Guidance Book”.

The two volumes, containing 500 career cards, are designed to serve as an
invaluable resource for students across the country. Each card provides detailed
insights into a diverse array of career options, catering to the varied interests and
aspirations of our young learners. I am confident that the “Career Guidance Book”
will be a source of inspiration and a guiding light for students as they navigate
their educational and professional journeys.

This initiative underscores our commitment to empowering students with
the knowledge and skills and to provide guidance necessary to make informed
decisions about their futures.

I extend my heartfelt gratitude to all the contributors who have worked
tirelessly to make this vision a reality. Together, let us continue to strive for
excellence in education and create opportunities for every child to realize their full
potential.

MESSAGE FROM THE DIRECTOR, NATIONAL COUNCIL OF EDUCATIONAL
RESEARCH AND TRAINING
‘hag
fo
The National Council of Educational Research and Training (NCERT), was established to
provide academic support to improve the quality of school education in India. It has been
imparting training to teacher educators, school administrators, in-service school teachers, as
well as guidance personnel through its Diploma courses for many years. Under the aegis of
the Ministry of Education (MoE), Government of India, we have also developed Tamanna -
an Aptitude Test for Senior School Students to enable stakeholders to know the aptitude of
the students of Grades 9 and 10. Students in the secondary stage (Grades 11 & 12) of
schooling have to take many important decisions regarding careers such as exploring courses
of study, preparing for competitive examinations and deciding the career pathways based on
their interests’ aptitudes and aspirations. Career guidance at this stage plays a very crucial
role in the life of the secondary students as they can make an informed choice regarding their
career, if they are well informed.
I am sure that this Career Guide book containing 500 Career Cards, which is being launched
on the 4th anniversary of NEP 2020, by the Department of School Education and Literacy,
Ministry of Education, Government of India in collaboration with the United Nations
Children's Fund (UNICEF) of India will set a milestone in the history of career guidance and
counselling. I appeal to Counsellors, Teachers, and Educators to utilise the Career Guide book
and help students to understand the relevance of their studies for future careers. I am sure
that the students will be able to gain insight into various career paths, understand the steps
required to achieve their goals and make informed decisions about their education and career
choices.
Director, NCERT

Choosing a career path is a big step for anyone. Imagine how daunting it can be for
an adolescent. Yet, many secondary students often miss out on essential guidance for
their educational and professional development. This information is crucial to help them
move smoothly from secondary school to higher education and into decent work. Career
guidance empowers students to discover their interests, strengths, and passions, paving
the way to their dream careers.
UNICEF’s initiative to establish 500 career pathways across the country is designed to
unlock students’ potential. Following the Ministry of Education’s guidance, UNICEF India
has customized career cards into two volumes. These have been compiled into books
in collaboration with PSSCIVE - Bhopal. These resources will be invaluable in helping
students make informed decisions about their futures.
The Ministry of Education’s push to integrate career guidance and mentoring at secondary
and higher secondary levels will further support students in navigating their future paths.
Additionally, the Ministry’s strategic move to deploy academic resource persons for career
counseling at the block level is truly commendable. This initiative aligns perfectly with
the goal of building a comprehensive support system that helps students choose their
career paths wisely.
UNICEF is delighted to support the Ministry of Education in this crucial endeavor, acting
as a guiding light for diverse professional trajectories and advocating for the right fit for
each student. We are eager to continue our partnership with the Ministry to ensure the
success and impact of this career guidance program.
Coptin Millay
Ms. Cynthia McCaffrey
Representative
UNICEF India Country Office
INTRODUCTION
Education aims to foster the overall development of students in all aspects - educational,
vocational, personal, social, moral, and physical - to help them become productive and
valuable citizens. Guidance and counselling, aligning with educational goals, strive to
maximize children’s personal development across all areas of life. By addressing the needs
of all students, guidance and counselling ensure that education is a meaningful and
fulfilling experience. These services not only assist students in understanding themselves
and others but also support them in handling personal, social, academic, and career-
related concerns. They help develop effective study habits, motivation, identify learning
or subject-related issues, demonstrate the relevance of school years for the future, and
build skills, attitudes, and interests essential for career choices.
The National Education Policy (NEP) 2020, introduced in India, underscores the
significance of counselling in schools as a key component of the educational framework.
It highlights the necessity of early exposure to various career options for students. A
career is an individual’s journey of lifelong learning, working, extensive training, and
acquiring new skills. Career counselling in schools is designed to begin at an early age,
aiding students in exploring their interests, aptitudes, and aspirations, and providing
information about different career fields and opportunities. The policy advocates a holistic
approach to career guidance, considering the individual needs, interests, and strengths
of each student. Career counselling should be personalized, helping students make
informed decisions about their future. NEP 2020 also promotes the use of technology
and online resources to enhance career counselling services. Digital platforms, career
assessment tools, and online databases offer students up-to-date information on career
options, college admissions, scholarships, and other relevant resources.
According to the National Curriculum Framework for School Education (NCF-SE) 2023,
the Secondary Stage should emphasize practical application. Students need to develop
a deep understanding of the vocation’s role in the world. Guidance and Counselling in
schools help students recognize and understand their abilities, improve coping skills,
enhance decision-making, foster better relationships, and become self-directed in
adapting to life’s challenges.
ABouT CAREER CARDS
Career cards included in this document provide a comprehensive overview of various
job titles, highlighting essential personality traits and potential scholarships available, in-
cluding details on loan provisions and expected income. They also outline the educational
pathways necessary for each career. The career cards also describe typical work environ-
ments and expected job growth in the sector or industry. Additionally, they offer informa-
tion on where to study and include examples or case studies of successful people in the
field, offering a holistic view of each career option.
UTILISING CAREER CARDS
Career cards can be a powerful tool for teachers ,counsellors and students to explore and
understand various career options. Here are some guidelines for effective use:
For Teachers/Counsellors
Understand each career card’s content, including job descriptions, required skills,
education pathways, and potential career growth.

e Define the objectives of using career cards, such as career exploration, skill

identification, or aligning interests with potential careers.
e Explain the purpose of career cards to students and how they can be used to
explore various career options.

e Incorporate career cards into interactive activities like group discussions, role-
playing, or career day events.

e Create activities where students draw a card, research the career, and present
their findings to the class.

e Encourage students to reflect on what they learned about careers and their interests
and skills.

Activities with Career Cards

e Career Mapping: Students create a “career map” showing different pathways to
achieve their career goals, starting from their current grade level.

e Role Plays: Students role-play a day in the life of a profession assigned to them
from the career cards, helping them understand the career’s responsibilities and
challenges.

e Career Fair: Organize a career fair where students present the careers they have
researched, using posters, presentations, or interactive displays.

e Peer Discussions: Arrange group discussions for students to share their learnings
about different careers and discuss similarities and differences.

e Skills and Interests Matching: Have students match their skills and interests
with the careers on the cards.

e Professional Talks: Invite professionals from the careers represented on the cards
to speak with students.

e Additional Resources: Offer further resources for students to explore careers of
interest, such as websites, books, or community programs.

For Students

e Use the information from the career cards to set short-term and long-term
educational and career goals. Look into job descriptions, required education,
typical day-to-day responsibilities, and long-term career prospects.

e Be active in discussions and activities involving the career cards.

e Inquire about aspects of the careers that interest you or seem unclear.

e Cross-reference information from the career cards with other reliable sources to
get a comprehensive understanding.

e Think about which careers resonate with your interests, skills, and values.

e Recognize the skills required for careers that interest you and plan how to develop
those skills through education or extracurricular activities.

e Do not hesitate to ask teachers, counsellors, or mentors for advice on how to
pursue your career interests.

An agricultural engineer is a professional who applies and uses the principles of agriculture and engineering to come
up with solutions in the sector. They design and develop new farming technologies that help in making farming more
efficient in terms of sustainability, safety and environment-friendly. Their roles entail designing, constructing and
modernizing farming equipment and machinery to improve land use, increase yields, and conserve resources.

aS cultural hub in Jharkhand. Her district holds a strong
= > position on the mineral map of the country which
o
OC 6 creates some fundamental problems for the locals.
A Basanti’s village, Lopongtandi in Ramgarh district is
only a kilometre away from the East Parej Coal
mine. For years she has witnessed how the mining
ie} 2 has left the landscape scarred and ravaged. She

heart. As a video activist and Community
Correspondent, Basanti is trying to highlight the
prevalent issues of her community.”

A Media Planner is responsible for putting in advertisements at the most opportune time so as to reach a large
audience in a short time with minimum expenses. A Media Planner has to ensure sound visibility of the product of their
client and also has to improve the image of that product. Media Planners also gather information regarding their
target audience and tailor the advertisements or promotional content to appeal to the same.

regarded as one of the most celebrated surbahar (bass sitar)
players of all time. She was given the name Annapurna by former
Maharaja Brijnath Singh of the former Maihar Estate, which
reportedly was a testament to her musical talent which was as
lofty and profound as the Annapurna mountain. Annapurna Devi
continued to provide music lessons for free, tutoring many to-be
et . legendary musicians. Annapurna Devi was a profoundly gifted
£& _ en artist. Her father and guru, Allauddin Khan, steered her towards

YY y extremely rare, even among aficionados. In 1977, the Indian
_\s government granted her one of its highest civilian awards, the

Akademi Award, the highest prize given to performing artists in

Public Relations Managers work on establishing and maintaining a healthy and long term relationship between a
company/organisation and its target audience/public/ employees/ investors/partners/ stakeholders. They are
responsible for flow of information from the company to the public and also carrying feedback from the public
back to the company as well. This involves writing press releases, organising fundraisers and events and
representing the client to company and vice versa.

A Publisher or a Publishing Professional is a person who is employed at a capacity to liaison with several media or
publishing houses and ensuring that the work of their client (an author) gets published. A Publisher can be a person or
a company. A Publisher might also personally edit, proofread and present the work of their client to the general au-
dience.

best version of the media to be shown on the screens.

A Textile Designer is an artist who creates unique patterns in textiles or woven clothes. A Textile Designer often
conducts extensive research of the market and fashion trends, and works with other professionals when creating
designs. They can also use computer designs in their work.

attract, engage, and motivate the customer towards making a purchase. Visual merchandising is a marketing practice
that uses floor plans, colour, lighting, displays, technology, and other elements to attract customer attention.

Tankadhara Mohanta comes from a financially weaker section of the society based in Mayurbhanj, Odisha. After
completing class 12 he needed to work to support his family and that is the time he came to know about the DDU-GKY
programme from one of his relatives. He then attended a counselling camp, which was organized by the SRLM in the
block called Rasgovindpur. There, he was counselled by the representatives of SRLM about the trade and job
opportunity available in different sectors and locations. In the counselling camp, he was made aware of the
programme & opportunities available to build a career as per his educational background and the course choices
available. After completion his training, he got his first job in a local Agarbatti Marketing company in Baleswar. After
one year, he got the chance to join MINISO, a Japanese Designer Brand, in Hyderabad as a Sales Executive in one of
the Retail Stores of MINISO. Within a very short time of just 2 years, he got 3 promotions and is now serving at the
position of an Area Manager. He is stationed at Bhubaneswar and managing 48 Retail Stores of MINI SO located in
different places of India earning 45,000 rupees per month.”

In finance, risk refers to the degree of uncertainty and/or potential financial loss inherent in an investment decision. A
Risk Manager's responsibility is to use his expertise to help his or her organization prepare for such uncertainties,
minimize threats to its interests, and reduce losses. The actions of the Risk Manager have a direct impact on the
company’s financial performance, efficiency, output, and overall success.

A Librarian is an information science professional who works at a school/local library/college/university/
government-funded library/private institutes and corporates with libraries. A Librarian ensure ease of accessing the
vast amount of information contained in books, periodicals and modern-day tools such as e-books, audiobooks and
video recordings and other digital sources. She/ he is responsible for managing and organising a library to easily
locate the study materials.

Linguistics is the scientific study of human language, from semantics to psychology and sociology. One of the main
jobs of Linguists is to document, analyse, and preserve endangered languages by conducting fieldwork and
establishing literacy programs. They work as a community to preserve a language.

role of the physical activity director for the school.

A Physical or Fitness Trainer helps people of different ages improve their physical health and fitness level
through exercise and healthy diet. They may specialize in one or more areas, including but not limited to
aerobics, weight lifting, pilates, yoga, and dance. They may lead group classes, provide one-on-one
training to clients, or a combination of both. A fitness trainer can also help alleviate muscle pains and
soreness which is common in players.

PRE-SCHOOL EDUCATOR
A Pre-school Educator teaches critical language, social, and motor skills to children who have yet to enter
kindergarten. Early childhood education focuses less on specific knowledge levels, and more on overall
skills that children need. A Pre-school Educator uses basic teaching methods such as showing pictures,
storytelling, rhymes, games, music, artwork, and play. They also teach the children good habits and
manners.

A School Counsellor is a professional who works in primary schools or secondary schools to provide
academic, career, college access/affordability/admission, and social-emotional guidance to all students
through a school counseling program. A School Counselor's role can be specific, i.e., related to only
middle school, primary school, or high school. It can also be general, related to all the students.

A Teacher is an educator who guides and nurtures children in schools and equips them with knowledge in
a variety of topics both academic and practical. A teacher can teach in preschool, kindergarten, primary
school, middle or high school. Teachers can also be special educators with additional training.

Special Educators are trained in communication with children with disabilities in a way that addresses the
child's individual abilities and needs. They provide learning assistance to students with mental, emotional,
or physical disorders by evaluating each student's progress and making reports for each of them.

translator would retain the concept and meaning of the original document as much
as possible.

A Bacteriologist is a specialized scientist who studies bacteria and other microorganisms. Bacteriologists study the
effect bacteria have on humans and animals, and try to understand their nature better and eventually use them for
human benefit.

Eligibility: Basic education (typically 10th-grade completion or
equivalent).
Duration: 6 months to 1 year, depending on the program format
and institution.
Diploma
Eligibility: Completion of high school (12th grade or equivalent).
Duration: 1 to 2 years.
Examples: Diploma in General Duty Assistance, Diploma in
Maternal and Child Health Nursing, Diploma in Midwifery and
Obstetrics.
Undergraduate
Eligibility: 12th grade with a background in science (Physics,
Chemistry, Biology).
Duration: 3 to 4 years.
Examples: Bachelor of Science in Nursing (B.Sc. Nursing),
Bachelor of Medical and Health Sciences, Bachelor of
Obstetrics and Gynaecology Nursing.
Postgraduate
Eligibility: A bachelor's degree in nursing, medical sciences, or a
related field.
Duration: 2 years.
Examples: Master of Science in Nursing (M.Sc. Nursing) with
specialization in Maternal and Newborn Care, Master of Public
Health (MPH), M.Sc. in Midwifery and Women’s Health.
Doctoral
Eligibility: A Master's degree in a related field (Nursing, Public
Health, Medical Sciences).
Duration: 3 to 5 years.
Examples: Ph.D. in Maternal and Newborn Health, Ph.D. in Nursing
Science.

A Healthcare Manager is responsible for the overall management of a healthcare facility. They ensure that the
facility is running smoothly, has enough staff, prepares the budget, and oversees the day-to-day functioning.
Healthcare managers work with a team with staff members responsible for different functions - admissions, billing,
HR and staffing, budgeting, performance evaluations, medical updates and so on.

Immunologist is a physician specially trained to diagnose, treat and manage allergies, asthma and immunologic
disorders including primary immunodeficiency disorders. These conditions range from the very common to the very
rare, spanning all ages and encompassing various organ systems.

Diploma
Eligibility: Completed 10+2 with any stream (some institutions
may prefer a background in sports or physical education).
Duration:3 months to 1 year.
Example: Diploma in Lifeguard Training (Pool Guard, Beach
Lifeguard, Life Saver), Diploma in Water Safety & Rescue.
Undergraduate
Eligibility: Completed 10+2 (preferably in a sports or physical
education stream).
Duration: 3 years.
Examples: B.P.Ed. (Bachelor of Physical Education), B.Sc. in
Sports Management.
Postgraduate
Eligibility: B.P.Ed. or B.Sc. in Sports Management
Duration: 2 years.
Examples: M.P.Ed. (Master of Physical Education) , M.Sc.
Sports Management.
Doctoral
Eligibility: Master's degree in Physical Education, Sports
Management, or a related field.
Duration: 3-5 years
Examples: Ph.D. in Sports Science, Ph.D. in Physical Education
Short-Term Courses: Certificate in Lifeguard Training (Pool
Guard, Beach Lifeguard), Certificate in Water Safety and
Rescue, Basic and Advanced Lifeguard Certification.
